And the migration is decided here rather than discovered: the moment the default lands, three populations coexist — real ids, the empty string on pointers dispatched since the field appeared, and no key at all on those predating it, measured at 3 and 8 of 11. Both absences mean the same thing, not in a wave, and neither may ever be grouped. Only a non-empty id forms a wave, asserted directly, because a rollup that gathers the empties would render one wave containing eight unrelated nodes — a fabricated grouping that looks exactly like a real one, which is the failure this whole section is named for arriving through its own migration.
Membership is open, and sealing is what closes itA member added after the wave opened joins it rather than forming a second wave; a wave with zero working members and no seal reports open, not closed; the same wave once sealed reports closed only when every member is terminal. Asserted in all three states, including the negative that an unsealed quiet wave never emits the closure signal.
The signal names the disposition split, never finishedThe closing line carries promoted, unpromoted and blocked counts separately, and a wave whose members are all terminal with one unpromoted is reported as needing attention rather than as complete. Asserted by test on that exact mix, since terminal-unpromoted is the state a coordinator misreads as done.
Unstaffed works, staffed adds synthesisA wave with no agent assigned still opens, seals and emits its closure signal; a staffed wave additionally produces one overview artifact enumerating every member's gate, quantitative measure, artifacts and follow-on entries. Asserted separately, so the signal is never contingent on staffing.
Wave cost is readable from the ledgerCommitted rows carry the wave they belonged to, and a calibration read reports node count, worker seconds and input tokens per wave without joining by timestamp. Asserted against the committed ledger rather than a fixture. A wave holding a shadow reports its durable cost with that shadow excluded and its measurement spend stated separately: a shadow is never merged, so it yields no durable node, and summing every member inflates precisely the cost-per-durable-node figure this measure feeds. Asserted on a mixed wave, following the precedent already in place — a shadow yields no rework signal for the same reason nothing can later re-touch its paths. Durability is decided by naming the exclusion, never by allow-listing the kinds: not (lineage and lineage.kind == "shadow"), not a list of the kinds that count. Measured on 797 committed rows, a third kind exists that neither this section nor its peers had named — lane-change, on 2 rows, whose work merges like any attempt — so an allow-list would have dropped it from durable cost and would drop every kind added after today. The accumulation key branches on the kind explicitly for the same reason: root_run_id is set on 14 of 14 redispatch rows and 0 of 21 shadow rows, so a universal root_run_id or run_id is right today only because shadows lack the field, and folds a shadow into its primary the day one gains it. One implementation, two callers: this row cites the exported predicate rather than restating it, because two copies of an exclusion rule is how a wave's cost and a per-node column drift apart while both look correct. Asserted per kind against the committed corpus, never against a fixture enumerating the kinds known when the test was written. The token figures a wave sums obey the same caution: on a compacted rollout the cumulative field resets, so on one measured rollout the field read 24,495,394 at its maximum against 744,077 at its last value — a snapshot that exposed the non-monotonicity, and cited as that rather than as current figures, since the file is live and the pair has since grown to 38,729,134 and 50,536,578. Segment at every decrease, not at the compaction markers. This section first recorded marker-based segmentation and that was wrong: the same file's counter decreases twelve times against three compacted records, so marker-only segmentation leaves nine uncorrected decreases as negative deltas, while reset detection at every decrease reproduces per-request ground truth within 1%. A wave cost summing that field takes the decreases as its boundaries. Two properties of the chain the row must not assume. A lane-change row is self-rooted — its root equals its own run id on both measured rows — so it is first-in-chain rather than a continuation, and anything walking upward expecting a parent finds none; previous_run_id is set on 14 of 14 redispatch rows and 0 of 2 lane-change rows, so the two kinds do not share that field despite sharing a root. And contamination is a different axis from durability, so the predicate must not absorb it: a contaminated row is non-durable today only because every contaminated row happens to be a shadow, which makes the composed predicate accidentally correct. A contaminated non-shadow cannot exist yet and may, and it would pass a durability test that had swallowed the contamination question. Assert the two axes separately, and assert their independence, because two conditions that coincide in the current corpus are the cheapest false proof available.
The closing line carries what remains, not only what landedThe closure signal states the executable remainder for the coordinator's target beside the disposition split, sourced from §14's inventory. Asserted by a test that the figure differs across a wave which leaves work behind: a value identical before and after fails the gate, because it cannot discriminate an exhausting wave from one of many. Asserted with plan-level readiness as the negative control, which read 7 before, during and after twelve promoted nodes.

§14 — A section authored mid-session is invisible to every readiness surface

Directed by the lead 2026-09-09, from the same halt as §13 and supplying the remainder that section's closing line has to carry. The two are one mechanism read from two ends: §13 owns the signal, this owns the number, and the number does not exist yet.

plan_section_anchors in _schema.py derives a plan's section identities from its gates and its comment keys, and its docstring says why — the derived identities let graph consumers validate staged refs “without adding another persisted field to plan state.” The consequence is that an authored heading nothing has yet written against is not a section as far as this system is concerned. Measured on the halted session: seven sections were authored mid-flight, six across two section families on one plan and a new leading section on another, and every one entered the working set because its author remembered writing it. None moved a number anywhere. The only surface a section touches is plan-impl, which the coordinator sets by hand, so its denominator is an opinion rather than a count — a plan can gain three executable sections and report progress going up.

The executable set is declared once, at DAG build, and the declaration is the coordinator's own classification written back. reckon-ship already requires reading the complete plan and classifying every section as implementable, deferred or done before it dispatches anything; today that classification is discarded the moment it is used. Persisting it costs one field and binds the count to a step that always happens, which is the property the alternatives lack.

The zero-new-field alternative was measured rather than argued away. Taking the remainder as authored anchors minus evidence-anchored comments agrees with the hand-set fraction on clive-local-workers — ten authored sections, evidence on all but one, against impl 0.887 — and it needs no authoring change at all. It fails at the tail, which is precisely where a coordinator decides whether to stop: on binding-rules-need-bound-checks both sections it calls unstarted are §1's diagnosis and §5's out-of-scope note, neither executable, and on execution-orchestration-hardening it reads three of four unstarted against an impl of 0.6. Over-counting is harmless in the middle of a plan and poisonous at the end, because a remainder that can never reach zero is a nag, and a nag is waived and then ignored. A declared set cannot over-count, and the gap between what is declared and what is authored is not noise — it is the mid-session signal the lead asked for.

Two independent repairs travel with it, both cheap and neither covered by §13. The view the closure fence actually names, crew(view="drain"), carries live_pointers, disposed_runs, unreconciled_runs, dispositions and runs — no ready-work field of any kind — so a mandatory drain-time check on it would have passed at the moment of the halt. And reckon-ship §7c states that both zeros are the termination condition; that sentence is true as written, and it is the sentence the halted coordinator was following.

MeasureRequired evidence
The executable set is declared at DAG buildA plan whose sections were classified carries the declared set, and a later reader derives the same remainder that classification implied. Asserted with the negative that matters more: a plan with no declared set reports an unknown remainder, never zero, since a missing declaration must not read as nothing-left.
Drift between declared and authored is the mid-session signalA section authored after the DAG was built raises drift and enters the remainder without anyone declaring it. Asserted on a plan whose declared set predates the section, which is the shape of the measured incident. Negative: a section collapsed after shipping leaves the remainder rather than appearing as drift, so shipping is never reported as new work.
Landing evidence is not completionMeasured on this plan, against itself, and it is the halt's own failure one grain finer. The first derivation treated any non-empty section comment as a finished section, so a section holding one landed node left the remainder while its other measures stood. With §13 and §14 declared implementable and one landing comment each, the remainder read 0 while eleven measures were outstanding. A remainder that over-reports is a nag and gets waived; one that under-reports is silent and reads as done, which is the reading that ended the session this plan was written after. So a declared section leaves the remainder only when its declaration is reclassified done, and the coordinator already has the beat for it — collapse-on-landing, where a section's body is replaced by its landed summary. Asserted on the measured case: a declared section carrying landing evidence and no done reclassification still counts. Negative: a section reclassified done never returns to the remainder when a later comment lands on it.
The remainder decrements when a section closesReclassifying a declared section to done in the landing beat lowers the count by one in the same plan write — the falsifier §13's row is asserted against, proven end to end here rather than at the signal. Negative: promoting a node that lands no section evidence and closes no section does not decrement, so the count tracks closed scope rather than dispatch activity.
The remainder is a lower bound, because that is what refutes a stopMeasured on the real project the hour the view landed, and it is the same defect in its third form. The project derivation returns unknown if any plan lacks a declaration, so at one declared plan of seventy-three it is permanently unknown — and unknown correctly never holds a fence. So the view moved from a false zero to an unknown, and neither can contradict a premature stop. The node's fixture had every plan declared and its measure was met exactly; what the measure lacked was the shape of the answer. A closure decision needs a lower bound: at least six sections remain refutes nothing remains, while the total is unknowable refutes nothing at all. So the view reports the summed remainder over plans that carry a declaration, beside the count of plans that do not, and the fence holds on the bound being non-zero rather than on the total being known. Asserted with one declared plan among many undeclared ones: the bound is non-zero, the coverage count is honest, and drained is false. Negative, and it is why the original shape was chosen: an undeclared plan must never contribute zero to the sum, since that is the false-zero this section began with.
The view the closure fence names can fail itcrew(view="drain") carries the executable remainder beside unreconciled_runs. Asserted by reading the view at a reconstruction of the premature stop and requiring a non-zero remainder, because the current field set is incapable of contradicting one. Negative: unreconciled_runs: 0 beside a non-zero remainder does not read as drained.
A coordinator can actually write the declarationThe MCP write path accepts section_declarations, and a write followed by a read returns the same mapping. Asserted by calling the write path, never by asserting the skill's prose carries the instruction. Measured 2026-09-09 and this row exists because of it: the field, its published schema and its HTML round trip all landed green while edit_plan refused the path with unsupported plan set path, so the instruction the skill gained in the same commit was unfollowable from the moment it shipped. The node's own gate had named the skill instruction as its reachable entry point and a contract test confirmed the sentence was present — prose asserting prose, which is the shape of every dead capability in this repository. The entry point is the call, and only a call proves it.
The termination condition carries a third zeroThe closure ledger's line reads foldable-remaining: 0 unreconciled-runs: 0 executable-remaining: 0, and §7c no longer names two zeros as the condition. Asserted against the skill text and a ledger fixture. Negative, and the one no existing rule catches: an empty ledger beside a non-zero remainder is an unfinished drain rather than a tidy stop — §7c's “folded with no node id” rule catches a falsified row, and the halted session's ledger had no rows at all.

§15 — Dispatch's refusal surface is neither predictable nor expressible

Measured 2026-09-09 while coordinating the two sections above. Both halves cost a wrong belief before anyone measured them, and both are this plan's own subject: a surface answering the half of a question it can reach, without saying which half.

A dry run clears a node it would refuse. The ship skill presents reckon crew dispatch --dry-run as the way to check a node before it enters the ready queue, and lists scope-conflict beside the eight contract properties in the same refusal table. The natural reading is that a clean dry run predicts a clean dispatch. It does not: the dry run evaluates the eight node-contract properties and nothing else. Established with a positive control rather than inferred. A node declaring reckon/cli.py while a peer coordinator's live run held that claim returned ok: true with the scoped property passing; and to rule out the reading that only foreign claims are skipped, a second probe declaring reckon/_schema.py — held by this session's own live run — also returned ok: true. So the claim registry is consulted at dispatch and never at dry run. A coordinator planning a wave against dry-run verdicts gets a subset of the refusal set and no indication that it is a subset.

The general form is one sentence, and the scope case is only its most visible instance: the dry run returns before the record exists, so it can never answer a question about a persisted field. It validates the node contract and nothing downstream of record construction. Measured twice more the same day, both times nearly producing a false report: a live path claim held by the caller's own run cleared, and the wave id assigned during record construction was absent from the payload entirely, so a working default and a broken one were indistinguishable from that instrument. Every field the record assigns is invisible to it, not merely the two anyone happened to check. So the remedy is not a longer list of things the dry run should also validate — it is the payload stating what it did not evaluate, which is the same requirement as the refusal enumerating the roots it checked.

A granted scope extension has no verb. A worker that blocks with a NEEDS-HELP: asking for one more path is behaving exactly as the protocol intends, and the orchestrator has no command with which to say yes. None of crew's verbs grants scope; --scope-changed on complete records that scope moved but does not waive the write-scope validator, so it answers a later and different question. The grant is therefore a hand-edit of the live pointer's write_paths followed by crew resume — a state the surface cannot express, which is the defect every-state-has-a-verb is named for, reappearing in the coordinator's half of the protocol rather than the worker's. Measured here on one node that blocked for a generated-schema artifact the repository's own gate requires be regenerated alongside the model it describes.

MeasureRequired evidence
A dry run names what it did not checkThe dry-run payload either states that live path claims were not evaluated, or evaluates them and names the owning run. Asserted both ways round, because the property is a distinction and not a field: a node whose paths are free and a node whose paths are held by another live run must be tellable apart from the dry-run payload alone. Negative: a dry run must not report a conflict dispatch would not raise, since one false refusal teaches coordinators to stop running the check.
A delta gate that cannot complete is named before it is demandedThe delta discipline assumes a base census can be taken, and on one file it cannot: tests/test_crew.py::test_first_dispatch_proceeds_with_a_freshly_armed_project_watch deadlocks, its main thread joining while its worker stays inside the watch loop in reckon/crew/runs.py. Measured twice — a worker's assigned pair terminated at exit 143 without producing failure identities, and the same single test terminated past 90 seconds on a checkout without that worker's change, which is what establishes it as pre-existing rather than a regression. So a coordinator demanding a delta over that file demands something unobtainable, and the worker's only honest moves are to block or to narrow the gate silently. The measure has two halves: a dispatch that names a file carrying a non-completing test says so, and the deadlock itself is fixed or the test is deselected with its cause recorded, never left to be rediscovered per node. The negative: a deselection with no stated cause fails this, because a quietly narrowed gate and a passing one are indistinguishable afterwards.
A falsifier states agreement, not a range, wherever a second measurement existsThe strongest form of the family, and it arrived by refuting a peer's own falsifier rather than a defect. A rule for deriving model time was gated on the derived share falling strictly inside 1% to 99%. Three rollouts returned 80.13%, 96.81% and 94.10% — and the worker volunteered that 144 of roughly 2,313 rollouts sit at or above 99%, which that clause called a refutation. It is not: a session with three bounded tool calls over an hour genuinely is almost all generation. The bound conflated the rule over-attributes with this session really was generation-dominated, so only its lower half was load-bearing. What actually validated the rule was an independent measurement of the same quantity: a computed span of 6885.08 seconds against the stream's own duration_api_ms of 6885.44, agreeing to 0.005% — which no plausibility range could have supplied. So a falsifier stated as a range on the derived quantity is weaker than one stated as agreement with an independent measurement of that same quantity, wherever a second measurement exists; a range is the fallback for when none does. This sits beside the verb rule and the absence rule as the third way a measure passes while proving nothing.
A done-when satisfiable by absence is refused at dispatchThe sibling of the row below and a distinct trap: not a permissive verb but an escape hatch in the evidence. A measure reading “every record carries a measured or explicitly-unmeasured value in all five cells” is satisfied by five dim dashes, so universal absence passes it. Measured by a peer over 803 committed rows when it turned this section's own generalisation on its own plan: the five cells are populated on 89.4%, 90.4%, 16.1%, 15.8% and 13.3% of rows, all five together on 3.2%, and any cell at all on 98.1% — so the measure could not fail on 97% of the corpus. An availability question wearing an existence answer. The check is the same shape as the absolute-green one: a done-when whose stated evidence would be produced by a system that measures nothing is refused, or accepted with the corpus coverage quoted back. The remedy the peer landed is the pattern to copy — a coverage floor asserted against the corpus, the remaining marker count reported as a number that must fall against the base revision, and width, alignment and marker-versus-zero left on a fixture, where those genuinely belong.
A done-when demanding absolute green is refused at dispatchDispatch already validates eight node-contract properties, and the ninth is the one this repository's own test policy states: judge by delta against a stated base, never by absolute green. A done-when requiring a test command to exit zero over files that carry pre-existing failures is refused, or accepted with the base failure count quoted back so the coordinator sees what it just demanded. Measured across one session's coordination: three of its nodes were gated wrongly by their own coordinator — a fence too narrow to include a generated artifact its gate required, a falsifier ranged over one field where the defect ranged over the declaration, and an exit-zero requirement on a base carrying fourteen failures. Each worker behaved correctly, one blocking and one refusing outright, and each cost a round trip. The negative matters as much: a node whose files are genuinely green must not be refused, so the check reads the base rather than the wording.
Granting a scope extension is a commandOne verb records an additional exclusive path on a live run, refuses when a peer holds that path, and leaves the widening on the promoted row. Asserted by granting, promoting, and then reading the widened scope off the committed record rather than the pointer, since the pointer is deleted at promotion and an audit trail that dies with it is the same defect as a note nobody can fetch.
An empty collection is distinguishable from an unasked questionA surface reporting no conflicts says whether it evaluated the question. Measured 2026-09-14 by the peer coordinator: dry runs returned an empty conflict list on some calls and a null one on others with no stated difference, and the scopes view called without a candidate wave returned both collections empty — because they are computed against a manifest that was never passed. So an empty array reads identically whether there is no conflict, the question was never asked, or the field is not populated on that path, and a wrong conclusion was drawn from exactly that and had to be corrected. Asserted by requiring the evaluated-question state to be readable beside the result, with the unasked case distinguishable from the empty one.
An exclusion named in a node contract is not counted as an inputMeasured 2026-09-14 while composing this plan's own scout: a done-when naming eleven peer-held paths so the worker would avoid them was refused for context-window-exceeded at 232,650 tokens against a 172,800 window, because the fit estimator resolved all eleven as named input files and counted 182,258 tokens of source the node would never open. The refusal named a true fact about the number and the wrong fact about the cause, and the remedy that suggests itself — a bigger lane — is the opposite of what the node needed. Asserted by requiring a path named as an exclusion to be excluded from the fit estimate, and in the negative by keeping a path named as an input counted.
A registry read carries the same binding rule the enforcer appliesA claim listed by the scopes view says whether it binds. Measured 2026-09-14: a claim whose worker died at launch having written nothing is non-binding by the dispatch rule — stopped and with no unintegrated work at its declared paths — and dispatch walks past it and records the disregard. The scopes view lists that same claim with no binding flag at all, so a worker reading the registry directly applies a stricter rule than the enforcer and blocks correctly-but-unnecessarily, which cost the peer coordinator a worker. This is the discriminating-value obligation of §4 applied to the registry: the value that settles the question exists and the surface drops it.

The refusal that the system's own advice provokes. Recorded 2026-09-14 from the peer coordinator: the recovery surface emitted, as its offered next action for a run awaiting review, a dispatch that the dispatch validator then refused — the review store lay outside the write roots the read-only tier grants, so a scope property failed on a command the system had just prescribed. The repair granted that store to the tier. The instance is kept because it is the sharpest available form of this section's subject: not a surface answering half a question, but two surfaces of one system disagreeing about what is permitted, with the operator holding the contradiction.

§17 — The runtime gate is a soft limit, and a soft limit that speaks to nobody is not one

Directed by the lead 2026-09-09: a worker is never killed at its runtime gate. The time budget exists to stop a runaway session, not to end a working one, and the response to an overrun is to message the worker.

Half of that is already true, and measuring it first is what makes the other half legible. Nothing in this repository kills a worker on its budget. terminate() has exactly one caller, the operator-initiated stop verb; the stalled determination at twice budget is a classification that feeds recovery routing and a worker-seconds source, never a signal; and the one killpg in the tree belongs to seat release and is guarded against signalling its own group. Observed the same day: a peer's node ran 28% past its budget, untouched and still logging. So the limit is already soft.

A live reader and a ledger reader see different schemas, which is sharper than a coverage gap. budget_overrun and budget_overrun_seconds are present on the live pointer and absent from all 800 committed rows, so a measure written against one surface is silently unmeasurable against the other. Neither half was findable alone: a peer's census found the absence from the ledger side while this session found the presence at the pointer side, and the discrepancy only appeared when the two readings met. A durable capture of one live instance is therefore evidence that cannot be reconstructed — recorded at crew/reports/soft-limit-live-overrun-20260909.md: a run 101 seconds past a 55-minute budget, process alive, still logging, untouched, and losing both fields the moment it is promoted.

What is missing is the message. An overrun today produces budget_overrun and budget_overrun_seconds on a record, and nothing delivers them to anyone — not to the worker that could land what it has, not to the coordinator that could decide. That is this plan's own defect at the runtime gate: the state is computed exactly and reported to nobody, so the only way an overrun becomes known is somebody looking. A worker told it is past budget can write the manifest it already owes and finish deliberately; a worker not told runs until something outside reckon reaps it, which is the outcome the directive forbids and the one nobody chose.

MeasureRequired evidence
An overrun says whether the node was too large or the lane too busyA budget overrun cannot distinguish those two, and tokens per second can — so a soft limit reading only the overrun blames a worker for the fleet's width. A large node generates a lot at a normal rate; a contended lane generates normally at a collapsed rate. One reading, two meanings, which is the shape of every defect in this plan. The instance is a peer's and it cost a wrong finding rather than latency: it attributed a threefold overrun to node shape, a 154 KB input file, and wrote that into a committed plan. The run generated 41,434 tokens at 11.53 tokens per second with seven peers on the lane; at the idle rate that work is 847 seconds, inside its budget. The overrun was saturation. The figure that would have said so was already computed by observe and already on the record, and nothing showed it to anyone — the same defect as the overrun pair reaching nobody, one field over. So the message names which of the two it is, and its threshold reads the rate beside the overrun. Asserted both ways: a node genuinely over-large at a normal rate is messaged as over-large, and a normal node on a saturated lane is not messaged as over-large — the second is the one that has already been got wrong.
An overrun messages the worker and terminates nothingCrossing the soft limit delivers a message to the running worker naming the overrun and asking it to write the manifest it has rather than the one it wanted, and the run keeps its process, its session and its worktree. Asserted with the negative that carries the directive: no code path signals a worker on budget, proven by assertion over the call graph rather than by inspection, so a future contributor cannot add one without failing a test. A second negative, asserted as its own case rather than implied: a fixture run finishing just inside the limit receives nothing, proven by asserting no message was sent. A limit that always speaks becomes noise and is filtered, and the test that catches that is the one nobody writes. The threshold and the pane read one shared fact, not two clocks: the accumulated wall and model seconds are exported onto the transition record, so this decision and the rendered cell consume the same numbers. The two are deliberately not merged — a display accumulation keyed on the lineage chain and a decision about one live run against its own declared budget want different denominators, and forcing them to share one would leave neither right. Same shape as the durability predicate: one implementation or one fact, two callers, never two copies.
The budget is denominated in tokens, because time is not load-invariantThis supersedes the threshold row below as the fence's denominator, and it rests on arithmetic rather than on medians — deliberately, because the first mechanism offered for it was withdrawn. One fact is robust across every slice measured: the generation rate varies with lane occupancy, 48.64 to 15.77 tokens per second across 109 rows and 28.23 to 15.87 across one day's. Everything else follows without a table. Seconds are tokens divided by rate, so a seconds-denominated budget inherits the rate's variance by construction and a token-denominated one cannot. Put plainly: a token budget charges the work, while a time budget charges the work plus whatever else the machine was doing. That holds whatever the medians turn out to be, which is why it carries the measure. A wall-clock ceiling still has to exist, but only as a hang guard, because a token budget cannot bound a process that has stopped producing. Asserted at two occupancies: the token budget's verdict on identical work is unchanged while a time budget's verdict flips.

The retracted mechanism, kept because it reached this record after it reached two readers. The first justification offered was that generation seconds is flat at 1.00× between concurrency bands while tokens fall to 0.70×, the fence holding generation flat by truncating the task. On 109 rows rather than 24 the assignment reverses — median generation seconds 876 against 2,605, a 2.97× rise, with median tokens 44,034 against 49,398, a 1.12× one — and both readings are confounded by node size, uncontrolled, biasing in opposite directions. So the load-sensitivity of generation seconds is not establishable from that data in either direction. The conclusion is unaffected and strengthened: generation seconds is the quantity that moved most, so a budget denominated in it is more load-sensitive than one in wall clock, not equally. A clean-looking 1.00× from 24 rows was taken as conclusive where the honest reading was that 24 rows could not answer the question, which is the mirror of this plan's usual failure — not a measure passing while proving nothing, but a measurement proving less than it appeared to.

If the fence fences on anything it is the retry signal, and it is currently silentThe danger figure is the one that was absent, and its absence is the finding. Across every run on the local lane in one day, at up to nine concurrent: zero genuine api_retry records and zero rate_limit_event records — discriminated on the record's own type and rejecting assistant and user entries, because a node whose subject is rate limiting quotes the signature simply by doing its job, which has previously produced 510 matches and none genuine. 24 runs, 22 gates passed, and both failures were shadow contamination rather than load. So the lane was never near its threshold, and a fence that fires on occupancy or on per-worker slowdown would be fencing on the absence of a problem. If anything is fenced it is the retry signal, and the honest current state of that fence is silent. Asserted in both directions: a fixture carrying genuine retry records fires it, and a fixture at nine concurrent with none does not.
The threshold comes from the agent-time population, never the pooled oneA peer's census over 800 committed rows settles two things this section had left to assumption. First, budget_overrun and budget_overrun_seconds are absent from every committed row — they live on the live pointer only — so a node reading them from the ledger measures nothing; the durable fields are worker_seconds, time_budget, wall_seconds and worker_seconds_source. Second, the distribution splits hard by what worker_seconds measures: agent time (stream_events, n=223) runs median 0.41x of declared budget with p90 1.79x, p95 2.97x and a 11.86x maximum, while wall clock (wall_fallback, n=482) runs median 0.30x with p95 0.86x and not one row above 2x. So STALL_BUDGET_MULTIPLE = 2 sits at p95 of the agent-time population — a defensible runaway threshold and far too late for a soft one, since by 2x the worker has usually finished or died. The soft limit is derived from the stream_events subset and the measure says so; around 1.5x lands near p88, where a message still reaches a working run with time to land what it has. The negative is the trap: pooling the two sources reports 65 overruns of 705 with p95 under 1.2x, which reads as almost nothing overruns and would justify no messaging at all — an artefact of 60% of the population measuring a different quantity. A threshold derived from the pooled figure fails this measure however close its number lands.
The overrun is visible where a reader already looksThe overrun appears on the monitor surface a coordinator watches, not only in a record a reader must go and fetch — the segment-time measure being built alongside this section is that instrument, so this row cites it rather than adding a second clock. Asserted by a fixture fleet holding one run inside budget and one past it, distinguishable on the rendered line.
A saturated lane is read from the engine's own counter, not inferred from the overrunThe first measure asks whether an overrun means the node was too large or the lane too busy, and there is a third state it must not collapse into either: the serving engine preempts and recomputes rather than refusing, so a saturated lane produces no error, no status code and no field a worker manifest could attribute correctly — only a run that takes longer. Contributed 2026-09-14 by the session serving the lane. The discriminator is the engine's own preemption counter read beside running-request count and pool occupancy, which is cheaper than decomposing a stream and is the only one of the three causes that leaves no trace in the run's own record. Asserted by requiring an overrun's attribution to consult that counter before naming node size, and in the negative by requiring an overrun on an unsaturated lane — zero preemptions, low occupancy — to still be attributable to the node. Three distinct mechanisms produced unexplained overruns on one lane in one day: a serving build whose prefix cache stopped hitting after the first third of its life, a router admission limit shared across every process on the host, and engine preemption under real residency. Only the third is silent by design, and a coordinator that reaches for node size without ruling it out is guessing.
A capacity figure names the workload it was measured onAny concurrency or throughput ceiling recorded anywhere carries the request shape that produced it, because a ceiling measured on the wrong workload is not conservative, it is wrong in the unsafe direction. Measured the same day and corrected by its own author: a ladder run on 200-token completions reached 1,536 concurrent without finding an edge, because those requests barely touch the pool — 26 concurrent sat at 3.4 percent occupancy. Read passively under real agent traffic the same engine showed 10 to 11 running at 41 to 48 percent occupancy, which is roughly 89,000 to 105,000 tokens per request and puts the honest figure near 21 to 25 concurrent sessions, two orders below. Asserted by requiring a recorded ceiling to carry its per-request token footprint and its occupancy at measurement, so a reader can tell whether it describes their traffic; a bare number is refused. The same rule the model-stamp measure states one section over: a figure that does not carry the conditions of its measurement cannot be checked against the conditions of its use.
✓ landed 2026-09-15

§16 — A finished run is reported dead, and the action offered is to redo it

Measured 2026-09-09 on a peer coordinator's run in this repository, read-only. Its manifest carried a clean status: complete naming three changed files and five added cases; its commit was already an ancestor of main; its worktree was clean; its process was legitimately gone, with the recorded launcher host matching the reading host, so the liveness verdict was sound rather than a cross-host artefact. The row read classification: abandoned and its next_action offered redispatch if needed. By the classifier's own documented mapping a complete manifest becomes completed_unpromoted, and abandoned is reserved for a missing terminal manifest.

This is the sibling of the defect that names this plan, with the sign reversed. The familiar case is finished work nobody notices; here the surface actively reports finished work as dead and recommends doing it again, which would have duplicated a merged commit. It is also the class of the park failures in §1: one field disagreeing with its authority rejects the whole reading, and the resulting state names the wrong cause. One symptom is recorded without a diagnosis, because the classifier was not read — the derived manifest_status in the live payload is not the file's clean complete but that word concatenated with the changed-paths prose. The manifest itself was checked and carries no run-on, so whatever joins them sits after parsing.

MeasureRequired evidence
A complete manifest is never classified abandonedA run whose manifest records a terminal status is classified from that status — the assertion is on the classification, since that is the wrong fact; the advice string is downstream of it and a coordinator who inspects the worktree finds the answer regardless. abandoned stays reachable only where no terminal manifest exists, asserted as the negative, because a classifier that never says abandoned is as useless as one that says it wrongly.
The fixture is the failing shape, not the shape that looks like itThe trigger is a key-value reader continuing a value across following lines that each contain ": " — the prose bullets beneath status:. The structural hypothesis was tested and refuted: blank-line count is not the trigger, because a manifest carrying one blank line above node: parsed correctly while the failing one carried two. A fixture built on the blank line would pass while reproducing nothing, so the fixture carries value-continuing bullets and the refuted hypothesis is recorded here to stop it being re-tested.
Two fields disagreeing is the cheapest detectorOn the same manifest, manifest_commits parsed cleanly to a single sha while the status field did not. So a terminal phase carrying a parsed commit beside an unusable status is unambiguous and needs no new instrument: assert that combination is never classified abandoned. This is stronger than checking either field alone, which is why it is stated as its own measure rather than folded into the one above.

The reader now refuses whenever a manifest status cannot be determined, not only when no field parsed at all. A body whose parsed fields are all outside the manifest vocabulary is prose wearing a key: value shape, and a status naming a word no part of the system recognises is refused naming the word and the recognised sets — each was once normalised into a mapping with no usable status that the classifier read as a vanished worker. The vocabulary lives in reckon/crew/reports.py as the single statement the reader and the classifier decide against; the dispatch template placeholder and recovery's own derived sentinel still pass through. Landed under commit 220848e. Full record: §16 landed.

The refusal above left one shape open: a body whose parsed fields read as manifest fields but omit the status key entirely. A review report wearing a node: identity line is exactly that shape — it parsed to the normalised mapping with thirteen keys and no status, which the classifier again read as a vanished worker. The reader now refuses it naming the missing status key, while a bare list-attribute excerpt (commits without a status) still parses and a body declaring a recovery artifact through a truthy derived field is never refused, so the repair does not block recovery reading its own fabricated manifest. Landed under commit c133d0e. Full record: §16 residual shape closed.

The refusals above are strictness for manifests at rest, and a live worker must never be caught by them: the unreadable reading is now gated on the process being gone, so a run whose process is alive with a fresh log classifies from that liveness even when its manifest parses to no recognised reading — both measured run-on shapes, one an unrecognised word, one a declared waiting status corrupted the same way, read running after this change where each read unreadable before. The dead control on the same bodies still reads unreadable, and a live run with a genuinely absent manifest reads from liveness. Landed under commit 8183a63. Full record: §16 liveness outranks an unreadable manifest.

The text reader now finds a status key through the Markdown decoration workers actually emit: dash and star list items, bold keys, inline-code values, leading indentation, and their composed form all parse to complete. The focused gate advanced from 53 to 59 passing cases with no failures, while the missing-status, unknown-word, two-keys-on-one-line, and literal commit-identifier refusals remain intact. Landed under commit 3833e84c. Full record: §16 decorated manifest field evidence.

The remaining emphasis placement now parses as well: the dash-listed and unlisted forms whose closing bold marker precedes the colon previously raised the no-field refusal and now return complete. The focused gate passes all 62 cases against 59 passing cases at base; all eight required line forms, the missing-status and unknown-done refusals, the two-keys-on-one-line refusal, and bracketed commit-identifier preservation hold together. Landed under commit aab1f788. Full record: §16 emphasis-placement evidence.

The liveness guarantee above now ranges over every manifest state a live run can reach, not only the unparseable-manifest refusal. Its last unguarded arm was the incomplete wait declaration: an alive run whose manifest declared a wait without its condition read unreadable, contradicting the stated classification order, while both sibling arms already consulted liveness. The liveness verdict is now hoisted once above the chain and both unreadable-producing readings — the refuse-to-parse manifest and the incomplete wait — consult it, so the guarantee cannot decay into per-arm guards. A valid wait keeps its waiting classification for a parked worker; an incomplete wait on a live run reads running with the refusal text still on the row; the dead control stays unreadable. The focused gate adds nine cases, 261 passed against 252 at base, with the identical single pre-existing failure at both revisions. Landed under commit 882012ef. Full record: §16 liveness outranks every manifest classification.

§18 — Runs ending together on one backend are one lane event, not N worker failures

Measured 2026-09-14. Eight clive runs reached a terminal state between 06:52:19 and 07:00:20, six of them within nine seconds, across four sessions. Every stream's terminal record carries subtype: "success" while its own text reads there is an issue with the selected model — it may not exist or you may not have access to it. Zero rejected rate-limit events, so it was never quota. The serving job had been replaced by a serve the router refuses, because the replacement's model cards carry no routing metadata block — that block is injected only on one of the two launch branches. The follower classified every one of the eight abandoned, the process is gone without a complete manifest, which is true of each process and wrong about all eight together, and it cost a peer coordinator an hour of hand reconstruction from timestamps, stream tails and the scheduler queue.

The discriminator is measured, and it is not the obvious one. The unrecognised-model line appears in stderr for 65 of 70 September runs, 59 of them healthy and committing work, so a substring search there finds nothing. The terminal result record's own text appears in 6 of 70: with the marker present 1 of 6 committed, with it absent 59 of 64 committed. Discriminate on the terminal record, never on the stderr line — the same shape as the local-lane saturation signature, where a node whose subject is the failure quotes its signature by doing its job.

MeasureRequired evidence
Simultaneous terminals on one backend report onceSeveral runs on one backend reaching a terminal state inside a correlation window are reported as a single lane event naming the backend and the member runs, not as N independent abandonments. Asserted against a recorded cluster of terminal records sharing a backend within seconds, and in the negative against terminals spread across backends or across the window, which must still report individually.
An unknown-model terminal is its own causeA terminal record naming the configured model as unknown classifies as a backend catalog change, distinct both from a rate-limit rejection and from a transport outage, and the reason names which. Asserted on the terminal record's own text, with a negative control on a healthy run whose stderr carries the same line — 59 of 64 such runs committed work, so a classifier keying on stderr fails this measure by construction.
A locally served lane is probed where it is cheapestLane health for a locally served backend reads the serving job's presence in the scheduler queue before a dispatch is attempted, and reports unknown rather than healthy when the queue cannot be read. Absence of a queue reading is never read as a serving lane, for the same reason an absent budget signal never holds a wave.
Recovery from a lane event is resume, not redispatchA run whose terminal record carries the lane-event cause keeps its session and its worktree, and the offered action is to resume it once the lane returns. Measured 2026-09-14 on the second cluster: all four affected runs resumed cleanly with every turn intact, and one held 87 lines of uncommitted work that a redispatch would have discarded. Asserted by requiring the offered action for a lane-event terminal to be resume while the session is recoverable, and by the negative on a run that genuinely died with an empty worktree.
The lane's availability is observed once, at the producer, and delivered to every followerThe project producer reads the endpoints document on its existing tick and emits a transition when the listed endpoint count crosses to or from zero, so a drop is observed once at the source rather than inferred N times from N sessions' dead workers. Asserted on the crossing rather than the state: a producer emitting every tick while the count is zero floods every attached follower, so the test requires exactly one line at each edge. The return edge is the half that pays and is asserted separately: a coordinator whose recovery is resume cannot currently know when resuming will work, because every one of its own runs is dead by then, and no session can derive the moment the lane returned from its own records. This subsumes rather than complements the first measure above — a cluster can be reported once because its cause was observed once, and the classifier has something to attribute the terminals to instead of reaching eight independent verdicts. Contributed 2026-09-14 by the third coordinator on this lane, who declined to build it on the ground that it belongs to this plan's subject; four followers were attached to the one producer at the time, which is the fan-out it serves.

Three instances in one day, not one, and the third is the sharpest. Contributed 2026-09-14 by the peer coordinator shipping the local-worker plan in this same checkout. After the 06:52–07:00Z cluster came a second at roughly 09:11Z and a third at 13:39:05Z that ended four of five workers simultaneously, with the endpoints document rewritten to zero endpoints at 13:39:06Z — one second later. Their stderr carried a healthy launch banner naming the model and the hardware right up to the end, which is the same trap as the unrecognised-model line: the launcher's own output describes the launch it made, not the serve that is answering now. Two independent sessions measuring the same shape on the same day, from opposite ends, is why the correlation window is stated as a measure rather than as a heuristic.

The cheapest probe landed while this section was being written, and it is not the scheduler queue. The routing pre-flight now reports a lane's serving state read from the endpoints document, beside the launcher-presence field, which stayed true through all three drops — so launcher presence was never the signal and the document always was. That satisfies the third measure above for a locally served lane by a better route than the queue reading it proposed, and the measure stands as written because its requirement is that an unreadable probe reports unknown rather than healthy.

§19 — Three verbs collapse into one, and the row that claims work landed carries no spend

Two defects at one place, found independently and confirmed to compound.

Disposition. Completing after a merge, discarding a dead pointer, and stopping a live worker all render as promoted, and only the first means anything landed. A peer coordinator discarded three pointers for runs that committed nothing and the pane reported three promotions. The disposition belongs on the ledger row as its own field — landed, discarded, stopped, superseded — with the row reading it, and promoted reserved for a run whose commits are in the ledger. This is the same defect as §4's one condition has one name, read from the other end: there, two names for one condition; here, one name for three.

Spend. Measured across the whole watch corpus: promoted rows carry zero spend facts in 90 of 90 cases. Not sometimes — never. Promotion removes the live pointer, and the spend derivation reads the run from the live-pointer list, so the transition is written after its own evidence has been deleted. Every promoted row records a folded run count of zero and a measured stream count of zero. The row that claims work landed is both semantically overloaded and empty of everything else, which is why a wall time, a token total and a generation rate are all absent exactly where a reader wants a total. The spend facts must be resolved before the pointer is removed, in the same beat.

Note before dispatch: this is a state-name change reaching promotion, the ledger, the transition record, the ticker and every test asserting on state names. The asserting files belong in one write scope, or a worker fences correctly and blocks — which is the measured cost of a verifier whose aperture is narrower than the change's reach.

MeasureRequired evidence
A disposition is recorded, not inferred from a pointer disappearingThe ledger row carries the verb that disposed of the run, and the rendered row reads that field. Asserted across all four dispositions of the same run shape: a completion after a merge, a discard of a pointer that committed nothing, a stop of a live worker, and a supersession — four rows that must render four ways. promoted appears only where commits are recorded on the row.
Spend is resolved before the evidence is deletedA promoted row carries wall time, a token total and a generation rate wherever the run produced them, asserted against a run promoted from a live pointer holding a folded stream: the figures on the committed row equal the figures readable from the pointer immediately before promotion. The present state — 90 of 90 promoted rows with no spend fact at all — is the baseline this measure is read against.
An unmeasurable figure stays distinct from a zeroA run that genuinely generated nothing records zero; a run whose spend could not be resolved records unknown, and the two never render alike. Asserted in both directions, because collapsing them re-creates the defect one layer down — a folded count of zero is what made the present emptiness invisible.

§20 — The row spends its width on cells that are never populated, and clips the one that matters

Measured over 366 watch rows carrying spend facts. Of the five spend cells, model seconds is non-null in 1.9 percent and the generation rate in 1.9 percent — two dead columns, ten screen columns wide, permanently rendering the absence marker. Charged tokens is 49 percent and the notional dollar figure 18 percent. Wall time is 54 percent, and on dispatched rows, where it is most populated at 84 of 100, it reads about two seconds: a transition into dispatched is at time zero by definition, so the figure is noise precisely where it is present.

Meanwhile every row is clipped by the pane, including rows whose reason is empty, so the clip lands in the free-text field carrying the recovery clause — which is the field §4 requires never lose its predicate. The inset constant is commented as provisional and awaiting calibration against a real pane; this is that calibration and it is wrong. Calibrate by measurement — emit a ruler line once at follower start and read where the pane cuts it — rather than by adjusting a guess.

The rule the module lacks is that a cell renders only where the row's state makes the measurement meaningful, blank rather than a marker, because the marker already means unmeasured and blanking noise with it collapses two different facts. Order the grid so always-populated fields precede optional ones: clock, role, node, state pair, model, effort, then the fleet counters, then the optional measures, then the reason last. Cutting model seconds, charged tokens and the dollar figure from the row while keeping them in the record frees eighteen columns and widens the reason from 57 to 75 at the measured pane width.

Model time is extractable and its scarcity is plumbing rather than measurement: one dialect reports an API duration on the result record directly, the other derives machine seconds from event timestamps with generation as elapsed minus machine. Both need a terminal record, so repairing §19's promoted-row spend defect is likely to populate model time and the rate on most terminal rows by itself — which is why this section is sequenced behind it rather than beside it.

MeasureRequired evidence
The pane width is measured, not guessedThe render width comes from a reading taken against the live pane — a ruler line emitted once at follower start, and where it is cut recorded — rather than from a constant carrying a provisional comment. Asserted by requiring the calibration path to consume an observed cut position, and by the absence of any width literal whose only justification is a comment saying it awaits calibration.
A cell blank where the measurement is meaninglessA cell renders blank, not as the absence marker, where the row's state makes the figure meaningless — wall time on a dispatched row being the measured case at 84 of 100 populated and every one of them noise. Asserted in both directions, since the marker must survive where a figure genuinely was not measured.
The reason survives the clipAt the measured pane width the reason field is wider than it is today and the clip falls after the clause naming the predicate. Asserted against a reason longer than the render width, which must lose trailing detail rather than its predicate — the same assertion §4 states, now with a width that can satisfy it.
A derived figure renders with its vintage and its denominatorAny figure the surface computes rather than reads carries the moment it was observed and the quantities it was divided by, because a derived number that has lost its inputs cannot be checked against the conditions of its use. Measured 2026-09-14 on the lane budget, which is derived from pool size and mean context: within one hour it read 37, then 22, then 15, then 143, as mean context moved between 15,300 and 144,902 tokens. Nothing changed but the fleet's own workers accumulating turns, so the figure falls under the fleet already relying on it and halves while an orchestrator is still acting on the previous reading. A budget rendered bare is therefore a number that was true and is not, in the same shape as the launcher banner and the endpoint catalog. Asserted by requiring the observation stamp and the denominator beside any derived figure the surface shows, and by refusing to render one whose inputs are absent — the producer already publishes both in a derived_from block, so this measure is about the surface not discarding what it was given.

Reason-survives-the-clip measure, landed 2026-09-15: the trimmed grid already spares the reason the whole trailing margin at the measured pane width — the 208-column pane this workstation measures leaves it 75+ columns, which is the deliberate widening this section set out — and the free-text cut lands after the clause naming its predicate. What was missing was the falsifier this measure asserts against, so the survival claim rested on a truncation test that proved only prefix retention. Landed under commit ba487aa as test_a_reason_clipped_at_the_margin_keeps_its_predicate_clause in tests/test_crew_ticker_layout.py, asserted on rendered rows at the fixed grid width: “the process is gone without a complete manifest for the archive dry run…” keeps the clause naming the predicate and loses only the detail after it, and a short reason renders verbatim with no ellipsis. Full record: §20 reason keeps its predicate.

§21 — The review is scored, stored in three durable places, and read by nothing

Directed by the lead 2026-09-14, on seeing a dimension score of 5 pass unremarked inside a total of 78: review is not just for scoring, we need to act on the scores. The state of the mechanism is worse than not acting on them. The only occurrence of a score being handled anywhere in this repository is the parser writing one — review.py:143, scores[dimension] = value — and there is no consumer at any later point. No floor exists on any dimension; every match for one in the tree is an unrelated context, burn or render-width constant. So a five-dimension score is emitted by a second reader, parsed, and written to three durable places chosen because each survives a different loss, and then nothing ever reads it back.

This is the plan's own subject at the layer built to enforce it. It is the same shape as §17's budget overrun, which is computed exactly and delivered to nobody: a measurement whose only reader is a person who happens to look. The review was introduced precisely because the previous signal had that defect in its stronger form — gate saturated at 1.00 on every lane, carrying no information and separating nothing.

Reading only the total rebuilds the saturated measure one level up. Measured on this plan's own first two landings: totals of 79 and 78, each carrying a durability of 5 against 17-to-20 on every other dimension. Averaging is what makes a 5 disappear, and a scored review that is consulted only as a total has re-created the single saturated number it replaced, one layer higher and at the cost of a second worker per landing.

A low dimension does not imply a worker at fault, and today's did not. Both durability 5s have one cause and it is the coordinator's: the nodes were fenced to an unversioned directory where the dimension's own definition — a test exists that fails if this change regresses — cannot be satisfied by any conduct of the worker. The worker wrote exactly where it was told; writing anywhere else would have been a scope violation. So weighting the dimension harder in a worker's brief aims at the wrong actor and invites either a compliant low score or a broken fence. The rubric needs a per-role definition, not a heavier weight.

The finding was right for a reason the rubric did not intend, and that is worth preserving rather than tidying away. The rubric asked whether a regression test exists and the honest answer for a document is no; what the reviewer actually surfaced was that an edited verdict or a citation pointing at a vanished line fails nothing. That was true, it was acted on in the same session by moving the substance into the repository's evidence record, and it would have been lost under a rubric that scored documents leniently instead of defining them properly.

Work generated here is a drain-queue row, never a followup. The rule this plan operates under holds that a followup on an active plan is hidden work: it renders on the page, satisfies the continuation check, and appears nowhere a reader looks to decide what to do next. A sub-floor dimension must therefore enter the same triage queue as every other discovered item, where it is either folded into a dispatched node with an id or exempted with a recorded reason, and where a complete pass cannot report nothing foldable while it sits unexplained.

MeasureRequired evidence
A score has a readerSome code path consults an attached review's dimensions after the review is stored, and a change to a stored score changes an observable outcome. Asserted by altering one dimension on a stored review and requiring a different result from the surface that consumes it. The negative half matters as much: today the assertion fails at the first step, because review.py:143 is the only place a dimension is handled and nothing downstream names it — so the test is written against the absence first and inverted when the consumer lands.
A dimension below its floor is not averaged awayA review whose total passes while one dimension falls below its floor is treated as a finding, not as a pass. Asserted against the measured pair from this plan's own landings — totals 79 and 78, durability 5 — which must produce a row rather than a clean promotion. The floor is per dimension and is declared in flight config beside the other gate settings rather than compiled in, so it is readable by whoever is deciding.
The row it produces requires a dispositionA sub-floor dimension enters the session-closure queue and carries one disposition from the closed set, exactly as a followup row does: folded with a dispatched node id, or exempted with its recorded authority, dissent, owner or context figure. Asserted by requiring the drain ledger's foldable-remaining to count an undisposed sub-floor dimension, so a session cannot report a clean close over one. It is never written as a followup on this plan, for the reason the completed-container rule gives.
The rubric defines each dimension per roleDurability means a test fails if this regresses for a node that changes code, and the record is versioned and something reads it for a node whose deliverable is a document. Asserted by scoring one document node and one code node against the same rubric and requiring the definition applied to differ; and in the negative, by requiring a document node that is version-controlled and asserted-over to be able to reach a high durability score at all, which no document node can do under the present single definition.
Each dimension names the actor who owns itThe review record states, per dimension, whether the score is attributable to the worker's conduct or to the brief, fence or write path the coordinator set. Asserted against the two measured cases, whose durability is coordinator-owned and whose scope discipline is worker-owned, and by requiring a repair routed from a sub-floor dimension to reach the owning actor — the roster member for a worker-owned dimension, the coordinator's own queue for a brief-owned one. Without this the floor generates correctly-refused work, which is worse than no floor because it spends a worker to learn nothing.
The dimensions reach the committed ledger rowEach reviewed run's committed row carries its per-dimension scores, so a score survives the loss of the config home and can be read from the repository by anyone with a checkout. Measured 2026-09-14 across all 854 committed rows in this project: zero carry any review or score field, the two promoted in this session included. Asserted by promoting a reviewed run and requiring its dimensions on the ledger row, and in the negative by requiring a run promoted without a review to be distinguishable from one whose review scored zero — an absent review and a bad one must not read alike.
A score is queryable against the job that earned it and the work it generatedPer-dimension scores join to their parent run and onward to the rows that run produced, so a question like which dimension was low on the jobs that later generated repair work is answerable from committed state rather than by reading files by hand. The join key exists already — every review record carries reviewed_run_id — and the reviewed side does not, which is what makes the join impossible today. Asserted by querying dimensions filtered on a parent run's attributes and requiring a non-empty, correct result from the ledger alone.
Two runs never hold one review record, and the check that says so covers the storeWrite exclusivity is enforced on the review store, not only on repository paths. Measured 2026-09-14 on a peer's fleet: two live runs declared the identical review record as their exclusive write path, and crew scopes listed both under one key while conflicts and live_conflicts both returned empty. The exclusivity validator covers paths inside the repository, and the review store is outside every repository, so the check that exists to make this visible never ran on the one store where it matters most. The asymmetry is the argument. When two runs collide on a repository path the loser still leaves a commit or a dirty tree, so the collision is recoverable and discoverable after the fact; in the review store the second write overwrites the first and the loser leaves nothing at all — and the record it destroys is, per the measures above, currently the only durable copy of a score. Asserted by declaring one review record as the write path of two live runs and requiring the second dispatch to be refused naming the first, with the negative that two runs reviewing different subjects are admitted. Note the benign route by which the pair arose, because it will recur: a review died seconds after dispatch when the lane went down, its worktree survived, a same-node retry was correctly refused on an existing worktree path, and giving the retry a fresh node id — the right call for manifest hygiene — is what produced two pointers against one file.

Of the three durable places this mechanism was designed with, one exists. The design named them and said why each was chosen: the record survives run-directory cleanup, the ledger row survives config loss, and the prompt in git makes the question that produced a score re-readable at the version it was asked. Measured today, the record and the prompt exist and the ledger row does not, so the stated redundancy is absent and the only copy of every score sits in a directory that is not a git repository — six records, unversioned, machine-local, and reapable by the same cleanup that clears run directories. That is the identical finding the reviewers raised against the reuse maps, one layer up and aimed at the reviews themselves: an unguarded record that nothing reads. The mechanism built to catch that defect currently exhibits it.

§22 — The lane's load is measured, pushed at the moment of dispatch, and never a cap

Every earlier section here is about a surface reporting something untrue. This one is about a surface reporting nothing at all, for the one lane the whole fleet runs on. Measured 2026-09-15: 37 live workers on the locally served lane across nine sessions and four projects, while crew(view="budget") reported that lane headroom: unknown, utilisation_pct: null, and dispatched normally, with the stated reason no rate-limit event in the stream yet. The reason is correct and the conclusion it licenses is wrong: headroom is a quota concept, an unmetered lane has no quota, so the only load surface we have is structurally blind to the only lane that matters.

The consequence is not a slow fleet; it is six coordinators reaching the same wrong diagnosis. Each saw its own nodes running long and had exactly one frame available to explain it — the per-row generation rate the ticker renders, a single worker's number with no denominator beside it. This coordinator widened its time budgets and recorded the lane as slow for the model. A peer read three nodes as slow for half an hour before thinking to measure its neighbours. Both conclusions were wrong the same way: a per-node instrument attributes a fleet-wide cause to whichever node the reader happens to own, and every reader owns a different one.

A concurrency cap is the wrong shape and is explicitly not the remedy. Ruled out by the lead on the reasoning that settles it: a cap counts live pointers, and pointers are not the contended resource. A run parked on a submitted batch job holds a pointer and generates nothing, so a cap starves the lane in order to protect it from work that is not happening. The mechanism to do this already exists and is deliberately left unarmed — _refuse_over_concurrency_ceiling is wired and every backend declares max_concurrent_runs: null. Arming it would trade a live judgement for a static guess, and the guess cannot be made correctly: one point on the aggregate-against-concurrency curve is all anyone has measured.

The right shape is a live reading the orchestrator judges, pushed where the decision is made. The denominators already exist and already answer different questions: _generation_rate in recovery.py:2715 divides generated tokens by model seconds, so a run waiting on an external job contributes no generation time and does not dilute the figure, while wall_tokens_per_second in _backends.py:351 divides by elapsed and answers how much of a run's wall time was productive. What is missing is not a measurement. It is the aggregation, and a reader.

Pushed, not asked for. A view a coordinator must remember to consult before dispatching is a coordinator-side convention, which is the compensation this project keeps measuring does not survive a context boundary. Three surfaces already arrive unasked and already carry the wrong quantity: the dispatch result returns a budget block and a lane_reading block on every call, the pre-flight returns per-backend hold state, and the follower ticker pushes a per-row rate and the fleet counters. The reading belongs in the block that already arrives at the moment of decision.

MeasureRequired evidence
The lane reports its offered load, and the report distinguishes generating from waitinglane_reading carries the count of runs actively generating on that backend and, separately, the count holding a pointer while in a declared external wait. Asserted by placing one run in a declared wait and one generating and requiring the two counts to differ, and in the negative by requiring a lane with no live runs to report zero rather than absent — a lane nobody is using and a lane that cannot be measured must not read alike. The waiting population is the reason a single cap was refused, so a reading that collapses them reinstates the defect in a new surface.
The lane reports achieved throughput, with its vintage and its denominatorThe reading carries mean and aggregate tokens per second across the generating population, the moment it was observed, and the number of runs it was divided by. Asserted by requiring the mean to change when a run is added to the population, and by requiring the figure to name its denominator, since a derived number that has lost its inputs cannot be checked against the conditions of its use. Measured today at 37 generating runs on the local lane, mean visible output 1.44 tok/s and aggregate 53.2 — figures that describe emitted text only, because this endpoint returns a thinking block at every budget and thinking is 83–95% of emitted characters, so a throughput reading that does not say which population it counted is not interpretable.
The reading arrives at the moment of dispatch without being asked forThe reading is present in the payload reckon crew dispatch already returns, beside the budget block, on every dispatch including a refused one. Asserted by dispatching and requiring the field without a second call, and in the negative by requiring an unmeasured lane to say so rather than report a zero. A coordinator that must call a view first is the failure this measure exists to prevent, so a test that reads the figure through the view and not through the dispatch payload does not satisfy it.
The per-row rate is never shown without the lane figure beside itThe ticker renders the lane's mean alongside a run's own generation rate, so the row carries the denominator that makes it readable. Asserted against the measured case: a row at 1.4 tok/s beside a lane mean of 1.44 says contention, and the same row beside a lane mean of 20 says the node. Today only the first number is rendered, and it was read as the second by two coordinators independently, which is the evidence this row is not cosmetic.
The reading is sufficient to route, and routing is a judgement rather than a refusalA coordinator holding the lane figure and the metered lanes' burn position can decide to send the next node elsewhere, and nothing in the dispatch path decides for it. Asserted by requiring a dispatch to succeed on a saturated lane when the caller asks for it — the reading informs, it never fences — and by requiring the payload to carry, in one place, the local lane's achieved rate and the metered lanes' utilisation and burn multiple, since a routing decision needs both halves and today they are in two views and neither is pushed.

A run that hits its fence, writes status: blocked naming the missing path, and ends its turn was unreachable by the one command that exists to grant the path. crew widen decided eligibility from the pointer's folded phase alone, and for a run launched through a backend that phase is folded from the stream's terminal event — a turn ending normally is not an error, so the fold wrote complete over the mirror of a run whose own manifest said blocked. The only discharge left was discard and redispatch, which throws away the session that had diagnosed the change. Eligibility now reads two accounts, the folded phase and the status the run's own manifest reports, and the manifest is decisive when it reports complete or failed: a run whose account says the work ended has no scope decision to widen for. A manifest read is gated on the freshness recorded at dispatch, so a terminal status left at the same delivery path by an earlier attempt is never believed, and an absent, unreadable or unsubstituted manifest reports no status, so eligibility then rests on the phase alone as before. The widening payload carries manifest_status because a widening authorised by the manifest emits phase: complete beside it and would otherwise read as a widening of a finished run. The focused gate moves 99 passed at base cdd0a1b0 to 105 passed at the change, exit 0 at both, six added cases and zero added failures; restoring the phase-only check turns the blocked-manifest case red (3 failed, 3 passed). Landed under commit bd10fb72. Full record: §22 widen reads the block its mirror lost.

§ Decisions

Where does a liveness answer come from?

The registry and the process table disagreed in a measured case. Deriving from the process on every read is truthful but costs a scan; reconciling the record on read keeps the cheap path and repairs drift; keeping the record authoritative is what produced the defect.

Locked 2026-09-03 by the lead. The record stays as the cheap path and every read checks it against the process table, repairing drift in place without taking the seat lock. This is the only option that satisfies all three of §2's measures at once: deriving on every read is truthful and satisfies the first two but leaves the stale record for the next reader to trip over, failing 'a desync is repaired, not merely reported'; leaving the record authoritative is the mechanism that produced the measured defect, where crew list reported watcher_live None while pid 85432 had been emitting transitions for twenty minutes. The accepted cost is that a read now performs a process check, and that the reconciliation path must never take the seat lock, so observing still cannot deny an arming.

What should gc do when the named project's checkout is not the enclosing repository?

It currently scans the enclosing repository silently. Refusing is safest but blocks a legitimate cross-checkout call; resolving from mounts is convenient but changes existing behaviour for callers relying on the default.

Locked 2026-09-03 by the lead. With no --repo a named project resolves the checkout registered in mounts rather than the current directory, which makes the measured failure impossible: three different projects returned byte-identical counts from the reckon checkout, and nova's 124 reclaimable worktrees were reported as zero. Refusing unless the cross-pairing is stated was rejected as the default because it turns every legitimate cross-checkout call into two steps and would leave §3's third measure declared and undelivered. The refusal survives where it belongs, on an explicit --repo that disagrees with the named project's registered checkout, which is §3's first measure. The accepted cost is a behaviour change for any caller relying on the enclosing-directory default.

Which Anthropic tier is the default, and how is the other reached?
